</div></div></a></div></div></div></div><div class="readable downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa para"><div class="TopicPara_topicDefinition__nKNQv"><p class="TopicPara_topic_explanation__Sp1wS themeLight TopicPara_topicDefinition__nKNQv undefined" data-testid="topicDefinition"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal disease is infection and inflammation of the periodontium. Key clinical signs are halitosis, plaque and calculus, inflamed and bleeding gums, gingival recession, periodontal pocketing, bone loss, furcation exposure, mobile teeth, and eventual loss of teeth. Diagnosis is by means of periodontal probing and dental radiography to assess the amount of attachment loss associated with the teeth. Treatment includes professional dental cleaning and cleaning of the root surface or periodontal pocket with gingival curettage (with or without creating a periodontal flap).</span></p></div></div><div class="TopicSubNavigation_ariaAnnouncement__P8fZ1" aria-live="polite"></div><div class="TopicSubNavigation_topicSubNavigation___jimK


      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87991997" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">A variety of terms are used to describe the procedures involved in oral examination and the treatment and prevention of periodontal disease. Colloquial terminology includes “prophy,” “prophylaxis,” and “dental”; however, these terms are fraught. More descriptive terms such as comprehensive oral health assessment and treatment (COHAT); comprehensive oral prevention, assessment, and treatment (COPAT); and oral assessment, treatment, and prevention (oral ATP) have been proposed. </span></p><span id="etiology-and-pathogenesis" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3261228" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3261229" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><section id="Etiology-and-Pathogenesis_v3261228" itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType="" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ" data-testid="topicFHeadSection"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Etiology and Pathogenesis" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Etiology and Pathogenesis of Periodontal Disease in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3261230" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal disease is caused by plaque bacteria accumulating on the tooth surface and eliciting a host response to the bacterial insult. The accumulation of plaque, the biodiversity of microbiota, and the host immune system are factors in the pathogenesis of periodontal disease.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87991999" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The oral cavity supports a rich bacterial microbiota of both aerobic and anaerobic species, many of which thrive in plaque on tooth surfaces in periodontal disease. Bacterial plaque on the crown surface of a tooth constantly presents antigen to the marginal gingiva, stimulating an inflammatory response, resulting in gingivitis. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992002" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The bacteria in plaque are predominantly nonmotile, gram-positive aerobes, including </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Staphylococcus</span></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> spp and </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Streptococcus</span></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> spp; however, many others are also present. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992005" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although this microbiota does stimulate an immune response, the bacteria in an otherwise healthy mouth exist in relative commensal harmony with the host. They may even be beneficial by helping to limit the numbers of periodontopathogenic bacteria. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992006" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">If the plaque becomes very thick (eg, with poor oral hygiene) and oxygen within the plaque is depleted, the bacterial population can become more pathogenic, with a higher percentage of nonmotile, gram-negative anaerobic rods. Along with many others, the following bacteria are found in the presence of teeth with periodontal disease:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992009" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Bacteroides fragilis</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992012" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Peptostreptococcus</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992015" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Porphyromonas gulae</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992018" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Porphyromonas salivosa</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992021" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Porphyromonas denticanis</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992024" data-testid="topicPara"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Prevotella intermedia</span></i></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable

      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992032" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Interestingly, some of the common human periodontopathogens such as </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Haemophilus</span></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (formerly </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Actinobacillus</span></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">) </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">actinomycetemcomitans</span></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> are notably absent in animals.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992035" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Subgingival plaque (plaque on the tooth surface below the gingival margin) is also commonly inhabited by these more periodontopathogenic species of bacteria.       downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992036" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Inflammatory mediators produced by the host directly result in bone and tissue damage around the root.       downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992037" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Development of periodontitis is also affected by other intrinsic (eg, genetics, tooth crowding, thin alveolar bone, age) and extrinsic (eg, diet, stress, concurrent disease, oral hygiene) factors.</span></p><section id="v87992038" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992039" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Plaque</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992040" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Plaque is the soft layer or film on the tooth surface that forms from a combination of food particles, salivary glycoproteins and minerals, and bacteria present within the mouth. It begins as a biofilm on the tooth surface that matures to a more complex layer from the interactions with the bacterial organisms found within the mouth. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992041" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Plaque is composed of many microorganisms that differ from their planktonic forms. In a biofilm, microorganisms are more resistant to antimicrobials, disinfectants, and antibacterial agents. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992042" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Plaque continuously forms on the tooth surface and in between the teeth. It can be removed from the surface by daily brushing and a professional dental cleaning, although by the next day, an additional layer has begun to form on the tooth surface. Plaque is the precursor to calculus (tartar). </span></p></div></section><section id="v87992043" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992044" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Calculus</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992045" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Calculus, or tartar, is the mineralized form of plaque. As the plaque remains undisturbed on the tooth surface, the salivary minerals help the plaque mature to form calculus. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992046" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">This hard substance creates a protective barrier for the bacteria. This shield can cause a mechanical irritation to the soft tissues of the gingiva and the buccal mucosa and also elicit an inflammatory response from the host, resulting in breakdown or loss of the periodontium (structures holding the teeth in the mouth). </span></p></div></section><section id="v87992047" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992048" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Gingivitis</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992049" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Gingivitis is inflammation (ie, redness, swelling, and bleeding) of the gingiva (ie, the gums), a tough, keratinized cuff of soft tissue associated with the teeth that is tightly adhered to underlying bone via periosteum and continuous with the alveolar mucosa. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992050" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In the progression of periodontal disease, gingivitis occurs first. Gingival inflammation then extends apically leading to loss of periodontal attachment.</span></p></div></section></div></section><span id="epidemiology" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992051" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992052" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Epidemiology_v87992051" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE

      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992107" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">any occlusion abnormalities</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992109" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">obvious signs of trauma to the teeth (tooth fractures or discoloration, etc)</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992111" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">evidence of calculus (tartar) on the teeth or inflammation</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992113" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">presence of all teeth</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992115" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">extra or missing teeth</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992117" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">evidence of foreign material or abnormal growth of tissue within the mouth</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992118" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The key to the conscious oral examination is symmetry. There should be symmetry to the calculus deposition or inflammation (if present) and symmetry to the animal's bite or occlusion.</span></p></div></section><section id="v87992119" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992120" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Occlusion</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992121" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">An animal's occlusion should be identical on the right and left sides. It is easiest to assess the occlusion on a conscious examination or on a sedated examination before an endotracheal tube is placed. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992122" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">There are 5 main characteristics of a normal occlusion:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992125" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Midline match:</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> Characteristics of a midline match are those in which the maxillary and mandibular incisors meet at midline on both arcades. A parallel line should be able to be created without shifting the line to either the right or the left side.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992127" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Incisor overlap:</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> The incisor overlap is described as the occlusal cusps of the maxillary incisor teeth being positioned just in front of the occlusal cusps of the mandibular incisor teeth so that the mandibular incisors occlude in the cingulum of the maxillary incisor teeth.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992129" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Canine interlock:</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> The mandibular canine tooth should be positioned between the maxillary third incisor tooth and the maxillary canine tooth. The mandibular canine tooth should also be slightly angled toward the buccal surface of the cheek.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992131" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Premolar interdigitation:</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> The cusp tips of the maxillary premolar teeth should be positioned in the space created by the mandibular premolar teeth. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992133" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Carnassial overlap:</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> The carnassial teeth in the dog and the cat are defined as the maxillary fourth premolar and the mandibular first molar teeth. These teeth should overlap in a normal occlusion with the maxillary fourth premolar tooth overlapping the mandibular first molar tooth on the buccal aspect of the tooth.</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992134" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Any deviation of these 5 points of a normal occlusion will constitute a malocclusion. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992135" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Historically, an animal's occlusion may have been described as brachygnathic or prognathic. This can be confusing because it depends on which arcade is the one being described as abnormal. Rather, malocclusions can be divided into 4 classifications:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992138" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Class 1: Neutroclusion</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> is described as a normal relationship between the maxillary and mandibular arcades, but there is malposition of one or more teeth.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992140" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Class 2: Mandibular distoclusion</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (parrot mouth) is described as an abnormal relationship between the maxilla and the mandible with the mandible being positioned caudal to the maxilla. This would give the appearance of the mandible being too short, or the maxilla being too long. In this occlusion, the mandibular incisors do not occlude with the cingulum of the maxillary incisors.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992142" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Class 3: Mandibular mesioclusion</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> (monkey mouth) is described as an abnormal relationship between the maxilla and the mandible with the mandible being positioned rostral to the maxilla. This would give the appearance of the mandible being too long or the maxilla being too short. In this occlusion, the mandibular incisors occlude in front of the maxillary incisors. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992144" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Class 4: Maxillomandibular asymmetry</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> is divided into three categories dependent on the occlusion presented:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992147" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">rostrocaudal</span></i></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> malalignment, there is a "front to back" discrepancy. One side of the jaw is longer than the other side of the jaw; stated differently, one side has mesioclusion or distoclusion.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992149" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">side-to-side</span></i></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> malalignment, there is a loss of a midline match, and the maxilla or mandible is deviated to the right or left side away from midline. The maxillary and mandibular incisor teeth do not align with midline.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992151" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In </span><i data-testid="TopicItalic"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">dorsoventral</span></b></i><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> malalignment, the maxilla and mandible do not meet to form a closed bite and the patient has an open bite. This is usually caused by the maxilla or mandible being either too wide or too narrow. This creates an abnormal space between the teeth that does not allow the mouth to fully close. </span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992152" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">An alternative term to describe any of these three types of class 4 malocclusion is </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">wry bite</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><section id="v87992153" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992154" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Anesthetized Oral Examination</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992155" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Anesthetized oral examination allows the veterinarian to evaluate the structures that support the teeth. It also allows evaluation of the other soft tissue structures of the mouth, including the tongue, tonsils, hard and soft palates, and buccal mucosa. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992156" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Anesthetized examination gives the veterinarian the best opportunity to evaluate these structures and observe whether there are any abnormalities present within the mouth, such as areas of inflammation, enlargement consistent with neoplasia, or foreign material within the mouth. Early detection of oral masses is paramount for detecting neoplasia. In addition, an extraoral examination including palpation of the face should be performed.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992157" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal assessment includes tooth-by-tooth assessment involving periodontal probing to measure sulcus depth and to assess furcation exposure in multirooted teeth and tooth mobility. Findings and any treatment are recorded in a dental chart (a graphical representation of the mouth, displaying each tooth in occlusal, buccal, and/or palatial/lingual aspects). </span></p></div></section><section id="v87992158" data-testid="topicHHead" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadSection__te7BY"><h3 class="TopicHHead_headerTag__TuL9A readable downloadContent 
        false
      "><span class="veterinary TopicHHead_topicHHead__Z_3lA false"><span id="v87992159" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadScroll__vc_gC">Periodontal Probing</span></span></h3><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992160" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">All dogs and cats should maintain a physiologic periodontal pocketing depth. This pocket is filled with crevicular fluid. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992161" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In a healthy mouth, the amount of fluid produced is very small and is meant to help flush food particles and bacteria away from the free gingival margin. In a diseased pocket, the crevicular fluid is considered an exudate composed of inflammatory mediators and the byproducts of broken-down tissues and cells. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992162" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">When advanced disease is present, an increased periodontal probing depth will be observed as will an increased loss of attachment of the periodontium (see </span><span data-testid="topicmlink" tabindex="0" role="link"><button class="TopicMlink_title__hb53t veterinary undefined undefined"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">periodontal probe photograph</span></button></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">). </span></p><div class="false" data-testid="topicImage"><div class="TopicImage_ariaAnnouncement__Q7eDv" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="0" dir="ltr" class="TopicImage_topicImageComponentSideline__iDQ4J veterinary undefined" aria-expanded="false" role="button" aria-label="Periodontal probe, dog Expand the Image"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageContainer__xGEDy TopicImage_topicImageContainerSideline__Gae5m  printBreakInside innerCurvedBorder" id="v87991827"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageHeader___E3ey"><span class="TopicImage_topicImageTitle__0Y8Op false">Periodontal probe, dog</span><div data-testid="expandButton" class="ExpandButtonComponent_veterinary__YQfRs ExpandButtonComponent_expandIcon__hjSiD false

TopicImage_displaynone__4Pxtz false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v113848344" class="anchor"></span><div class="para"> <p>To assess the probing depth on a patient, a periodontal probe is inserted with gentle pressure between the free gingival margin of the gingiva and the tooth surface to the level where the gingiva is attached to the tooth. The depth of penetration directly corresponds with the periodontal probing depth for that area of a tooth. Because a single area may not be representative, six different areas need to be assessed for circumferential evaluation of a tooth. The normal periodontal probing depth is </p></div></div><div class="TopicImage_description__n9qkZ false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v87991831" class="anchor" ></span><div class="para" > <p >Courtesy of Dr. Brenda Mulherin.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992165" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The normal accepted probing depth for the dog is 1–3 mm and for the cat is 0.5–1 mm. Although the periodontal probing depth can vary between patients and specific teeth, 1–3 mm is the accepted standard range regardless of the size of the patient. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992166" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal probing is accomplished by placing the periodontal probe between the tooth and the free gingival margin and sinking the probe into the sulcus with gentle pressure. The periodontal probing depth is calculated as the measurement where the probe stops within the base of the sulcus (ie, the level where the gingiva is attached to the tooth).</span></p></div></section><section id="v87992167" data-testid="topicHHead" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadSection__te7BY"><h3 class="TopicHHead_headerTag__TuL9A readable downloadContent 
        false
      "><span class="veterinary TopicHHead_topicHHead__Z_3lA false"><span id="v87992168" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadScroll__vc_gC">Furcation Exposure</span></span></h3><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992169" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">For multirooted teeth, the periodontal probe is inserted from the side where the roots branch to assess for furcation exposure.</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992172" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 1 furcation involvement exists when a periodontal probe extends less than halfway under the crown in any direction of a multirooted tooth with associated attachment loss. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992174" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 2 furcation involvement exists when a periodontal probe extends approximately halfway under the crown of a multirooted tooth with associated attachment loss but does not extend all the way through the roots of a tooth.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992176" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 3 furcation exposure exists when a periodontal probe extends under the crown of a multirooted tooth, through and through from one side of the furcation and out the other.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><section id="v87992177" data-testid="topicHHead" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadSection__te7BY"><h3 class="TopicHHead_headerTag__TuL9A readable downloadContent 
        false
      "><span class="veterinary TopicHHead_topicHHead__Z_3lA false"><span id="v87992178" class="TopicHHead_topicHHeadScroll__vc_gC">Mobility</span></span></h3><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992179" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">All teeth should have some level of mobility. If they were held rigidly in the alveolar bone, they would be more prone to trauma and fracture. The periodontal ligament allows physiologic movement of the teeth. </span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992182" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 0 mobility up to 0.2 mm is considered physiologic. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992184" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 1 mobility is present when tooth mobility is increased in any direction other than axial over a distance of > 0.2 mm and up to 0.5 mm. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992186" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 2 mobility is present when tooth mobility is increased in any direction other than axial over a distance of > 0.5 mm and up to 1 mm. </span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992188" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Stage 3 mobility is present when tooth mobility is increased in any direction other than axial over a distance > 1 mm or any axial movement. An axial movement would be one in which the tooth can be depressed into the alveolus in an axial direction. </span></p></li></ul></div></section><section id="v87992189" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992190" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Intraoral Dental Radiography</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992191" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The </span><span><a class="TopicLink_topicLink__qJmYk veterinary undefined" href="https://www.aaha.org/aaha-guidelines/dental-care/dental-care-home/" target="" rel="" data-testid="topicLink">2019 AAHA Dental Care Guidelines for Dogs and Cats</a></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> strongly recommends full-mouth intraoral dental radiography in all dental patients. Intraoral radiographs are necessary to fully assess the extent of disease and thus avoid inappropriate treatment or overlooking inapparent disease (see </span><span data-testid="topicmlink" tabindex="0" role="link"><button class="TopicMlink_title__hb53t veterinary undefined undefined"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">periodontitis radiograph</span></button></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">).</span></p><div class="false" data-testid="topicImage"><div class="TopicImage_ariaAnnouncement__Q7eDv" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="0" dir="ltr" class="TopicImage_topicImageComponentSideline__iDQ4J veterinary undefined" aria-expanded="false" role="button" aria-label="Periodontitis, radiograph, cat Expand the Image"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageContainer__xGEDy TopicImage_topicImageContainerSideline__Gae5m  printBreakInside innerCurvedBorder" id="v4731632"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageHeader___E3ey"><span class="TopicImage_topicImageTitle__0Y8Op false">Periodontitis, radiograph, cat</span><div data-testid="expandButton" class="ExpandButtonComponent_veterinary__YQfRs ExpandButtonComponent_expandIcon__hjSiD false

      "><div class="TopicThumbnail_widget__btz5q"><div class="TopicThumbnail_topicImages__hyBLq"><span           veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Treatment" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Treatment of Periodontal Disease in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992198" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Professional dental cleaning</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992200" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal treatment</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992202" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Dental extraction</span></p></li></ul><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992203" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Treatment of periodontal disease depends on the severity of attachment loss. Frequent treatments that are used to stabilize or treat the progression of disease include the following:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992208" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">professional anesthetized dental cleaning (scaling and polishing)</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992210" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">closed periodontal treatment (root planing and gingival curettage)</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992212" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">local administration of antiseptic and anti-inflammatory drugs</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992214" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">open periodontal treatment (improved access to diseased tissues after flap creation)</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992216" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">complex flaps and bone grafting procedures to extraction of nonsalvageable teeth</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992218" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">home oral hygiene (measures taken by the client at home)</span></p></li></ul><section id="v87992219" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992220" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Professional Dental Cleaning</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992221" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">When treating gingivitis, removal of the bacterial plaque on the tooth surfaces is of utmost importance. This can reverse gingivitis, returning the gingiva to a healthy, uninflamed state. This is achieved through professional dental cleaning (scaling and polishing) with power and manual instruments under general anesthesia to remove plaque and calculus. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992222" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Dental cleaning on an awake animal improves the cosmetic appearance of the tooth crowns but does not improve periodontal health. The owner might falsely believe the condition has been treated; however, periodontal disease continues to thrive below the free gingival margin, causing attachment loss. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992223" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">If the gingivitis does not resolve, further examination should be performed to identify additional complicating conditions, such as persistent subgingival plaque and calculus or the presence of predisposing factors.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992224" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Some less common causes of gingivitis, such as systemic disease (eg, </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/diseases-of-the-mouth-in-small-animals/oral-inflammatory-and-ulcerative-disease-in-small-animals" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">uremic stomatitis</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">), autoimmune disease, juvenile hyperplastic gingivitis, or gingivostomatitis, may require more than plaque removal alone to decrease the inflammation.</span></p></div></section><section id="v87992226" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType=""></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType=""></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992227" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Periodontal Treatment</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992228" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontitis requires more aggressive periodontal treatment. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992229" data-testid="topicPara"><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Closed root planing</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> and curettage is debridement without creating a mucoperiosteal flap. </span><b class="TopicPara_topicBold__4Dpr9" data-testid="topicBold"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Open root planing</span></b><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> is the act of cleaning and debriding the root surface or periodontal pocket after creating a mucogingival flap. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992230" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Various periodontal surgeries with or without bone graft or membrane placement can also be used to help decrease attachment loss noted on anesthetized oral examination. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992231" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Root scaling (removing plaque and calculus on exposed root surfaces) and root planing (smoothing the root surfaces by removing textural irregularities and diseased cementum) are performed, followed by gingival curettage that removes the infected and inflamed inside layer of a periodontal pocket. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992232" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Shallow periodontal pockets are treated in a closed fashion (without a flap); however, pockets > 5–6 mm deep require open surgery (creation of a periodontal flap) to expose the root surface and alveolar bone for adequate treatment (root scaling and planing and alveoloplasty). The connective tissue side of the flap needs to be debrided before closure of the flap to avoid contact of infected and inflamed granulation tissue with the planed root surfaces. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992233" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Local placement of a perioceutic agent containing antimicrobials (eg, doxycycline) into cleaned periodontal pockets may be helpful. Extraction is often the best treatment for teeth with increased mobility and attachment loss that have a guarded to poor prognosis. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992234" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontitis is not as easily reversible as gingivitis. Lost alveolar bone may be augmented by use of bone grafts or bone graft substitutes. Barrier membranes may be placed between the bone defect and gingival tissues to achieve guided tissue regeneration. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992235" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Maxillary canine teeth with pockets on the palatal aspect of the tooth that have already progressed to form an oronasal fistula require extraction and oronasal fistula repair. Deep infrabony defects in multirooted teeth with bone loss that undermines a furcation can infect the pulp through a furcation canal, resulting in secondary endodontic disease. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992236" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Similarly, if periodontitis has progressed apically and reached the apex of the root of a tooth, secondary endodontic disease will develop. Saving such teeth requires both periodontal treatment and </span><span data-testid="topicXlink"><span><a class="TopicXLink_title__Oh7al veterinary undefined" href="/digestive-system/dentistry-in-small-animals/endodontic-disease-in-small-animals" data-testid="topic-xlink-anchor"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">endodontic treatment</span></a></span></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">, and the prognosis is determined by the extent of periodontal disease.</span></p></div></section><section id="v87992238" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSection__knUIS" data-testid="topicGHead"><span></span><div class=""><h3 class="TopicGHead_headerTag__E1d_E readable downloadContent false"><span></span><span class="TopicGHead_topicGHead__avdGK"><span id="v87992239" class="TopicGHead_topicGHeadSectionWithScroll__rJBcG">Dental Extraction</span></span></h3></div><div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992240" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Teeth that have become mobile because of loss of attachment should be extracted. Occasionally teeth can be saved through major periodontal surgery procedures. It is important to note that disease will recur without drastic changes in home oral hygiene. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992241" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Extraction of diseased teeth allows the soft tissue and affected bone to heal. While this procedure is a definitive treatment for a diseased tooth, a pet dog or cat can function without teeth and have an excellent and potentially improved quality of life.</span></p></div></section></div></section><span id="prevention" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3261252" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><span id="v3261253" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor"></span><section id="Prevention_v3261252" itemProp="" itemscope="" itemType="" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ" data-testid="topicFHeadSection"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Prevention" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Prevention of Periodontal Disease in Small Animals</span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3261255" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Prevention of gingivitis is the same as its treatment: plaque removal and control. Biofilms are easily and effectively removed mechanically with a toothbrush. Even large accumulations of supragingival plaque are easily removed by toothbrushing. Teeth should be brushed daily to remove plaque and prevent calculus (tartar) accumulation (see </span><span data-testid="topicmlink" tabindex="0" role="link"><button class="TopicMlink_title__hb53t veterinary undefined undefined"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">plaque light photograph</span></button></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">). While plaque can be removed by brushing, calculus can only be removed with professional scaling and polishing.</span></p><div class="false" data-testid="topicImage"><div class="TopicImage_ariaAnnouncement__Q7eDv" aria-live="polite"></div><div tabindex="0" dir="ltr" class="TopicImage_topicImageComponentSideline__iDQ4J veterinary undefined" aria-expanded="false" role="button" aria-label="Plaque light, dog Expand the Image"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageContainer__xGEDy TopicImage_topicImageContainerSideline__Gae5m  printBreakInside innerCurvedBorder" id="v87991834"><div class="TopicImage_topicImageHeader___E3ey"><span class="TopicImage_topicImageTitle__0Y8Op false">Plaque light, dog</span><div data-testid="expandButton" class="ExpandButtonComponent_veterinary__YQfRs ExpandButtonComponent_expandIcon__hjSiD false
      undefined "></div></div><div class="TopicImage_imageThumbnail__a8iqR false"><div class="TopicImage_imageThumbnailContainer__sSacK" tabindex="-1"><div class="TopicImage_thumnailContainer__QOC6J"><div class="TopicThumbnail_TopicThumbnail__GmnUB"><div class="TopicThumbnail_featuredWidget__CSiGc TopicThumbnail_featuredWidgetColor__gBXhd
      "><div class="TopicThumbnail_widget__btz5q"><div class="TopicThumbnail_topicImages__hyBLq"><span style="box-sizing:border-box;display:inline-block;overflow:hidden;width:initial;height:initial;background:none;opacity:1;border:0;margin:0;padding:0;position:relative;max-width:100%"><span style="position:absolute;top:0;left:0;bottom:0;right:0;box-sizing:border-box;padding:0;border:none;margin:auto;display:block;width:0;height:0;min-width:100%;max-width:100%;min-height:100%;max-height:100%"/><noscript><img alt="" srcSet="https://edge.sitecorecloud.io/mmanual-ssq1ci05/media/veterinary/images/plaque-light-dog-mulherin-sized.jpg?mw=1080&amp;thn=0&amp;sc_lang=en 1x, https://edge.sitecorecloud.io/mmanual-ssq1ci05/media/veterinary/images/plaque-light-dog-mulherin-sized.jpg?mw=1920&amp;thn=0&amp;sc_lang=en 2x" src="https://edge.sitecorecloud.io/mmanual-ssq1ci05/media/veterinary/images/plaque-light-dog-mulherin-sized.jpg?mw=1920&amp;thn=0&amp;sc_lang=en" decoding="async" data-nimg="intrinsic" style="position:absolute;top:0;left:0;bottom:0;right:0;box-sizing:border-box;padding:0;border:none;margin:auto;display:block;width:0;height:0;min-width:100%;max-width:100%;min-height:100%;max-height:100%" loading="lazy"/></noscript></span></div><div class="TopicThumbnail_tag__WXR4w undefined">Image</div></div></div></div></div><div class="TopicImage_mediaPrintDescription__giskr TopicImage_displaynone__4Pxtz false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v113848352" class="anchor"></span><div class="para"> <p>Use of a plaque light on the maxillary premolar teeth of a dog. Plaque fluoresces a bright pink color when the blue light is applied. This can be a training tool for owners to recognize the amount of plaque on the tooth surface. In addition, for owners who are diligent with home care, it can identify areas in which they could improve their brushing technique. Note that the image demonstrates the use of a plaque light to identify plaque, but it is overlying a heavy layer of calculus, which can only be removed by a professional.</p></div></div><div class="TopicImage_description__n9qkZ false" tabindex="-1"><span id="v87991838" class="anchor" ></span><div class="para" > <p >Courtesy of Dr. Brenda Mulherin.</p></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992245" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Only the outside (labial and buccal) surfaces of the teeth need to be brushed in most dogs and cats. The lingual or palatal surfaces of the teeth are more difficult to access. Fortunately, the tongue rubs against these surfaces, allowing less plaque and calculus formation on these surfaces in most patients. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992246" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">For dogs and cats that may not tolerate regular toothbrushing, plaque accumulation can be decreased by wiping the tooth surfaces with a gauze pad at least every second day. Plaque that remains on the tooth surface for > 2 days mineralizes to form calculus that cannot be removed by brushing. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992247" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Although calculus gives the appearance of unhealthy teeth, its contribution to periodontal disease is minor. Calculus needs to be removed with a professional dental cleaning and cannot be removed with daily brushing. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3261256" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Texture of the diet, chews, treats, and toys can affect the self-cleansing mechanisms of the teeth. Firm, fibrous items that allow tooth penetration can remove plaque (and occasionally calculus) from the tooth surfaces during chewing. Items that are hard (eg, bones, hooves) can crack teeth and should be avoided.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992248" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">The common perception that dry kibble (as opposed to canned food) decreases plaque and calculus through mechanical cleaning does not necessarily always hold true. The </span><span><a class="TopicLink_topicLink__qJmYk veterinary undefined" href="https://www.vohc.org" target="" rel="" data-testid="topicLink">Veterinary Oral Health Council website</a></span><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C"> provides further information about dental home care products that meet certain requirements for plaque and calculus control.</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992250" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">In addition to texture, some diets are formulated to include ingredients that help decrease oral bacteria (eg, chlorhexidine) or slow plaque mineralization (eg, polyphosphates).</span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3261257" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Products that slow the development of the pellicle (biofilm) on the tooth surface or prevent the adhesion of pioneering plaque bacteria on the surface of the tooth may provide some benefit. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v3261258" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Prevention of periodontitis is more complicated. Regular daily home care and oral hygiene measures to prevent and remove supragingival plaque provide some protection. This routine helps by minimizing the number of periodontopathogens in the mouth. </span></p><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992251" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">More importantly, predisposing anatomical and systemic factors, including the following, should be identified and addressed:</span></p><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992254" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Severe crowding of teeth or supernumerary teeth causing crowding can be relieved through selective surgical extractions.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992256" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Metabolic conditions such as diabetes mellitus or renal failure can be diagnosed, treated, and controlled.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992258" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Inappropriate behaviors or parafunctional habits such as cage biting or rock chewing and inappropriate selection of toys (eg, tennis balls) that damage the tissues can be identified and modified.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><span id="key-points" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992259" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992260" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="Key-Points_v87992259" class="TopicFHead_showKeyPoints__RB4kR veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 id="KeyPoints_v87992260" class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
          veterinary sticky-fhead readable downloadContent  undefined" data-originaltitle="Key Points" data-testid="topicFHeadHeading"><span class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadTitle__6uLUl"><span>Key Points</span><span class="TopicFHead_keypoints__9WCzn"></span></span><span class="hidden-span"></span></h2><div class="TopicFHead_fHeadBody__wxFX8" data-testid="fheadbody"><ul class="TopicList_topicList__nW6kG undefined
            undefined undefined" data-testid="topiclistList"><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992263" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Plaque is a soft biofilm that, combined with the host immune response, produces inflammation of the periodontium, resulting in periodontal disease.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992265" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Periodontal disease is diagnosed and treated under general anesthesia by trained veterinary professionals using periodontal probing and dental radiography to assess attachment loss.</span></p></li><li data-testid="topicListItem"><p class=" TopicPara_topicParaWithoutScroll__yzic5 readable 
      downloadContent TopicPara_topicPara__06ZHa TopicPara_inlineSpacingFix__tKAA9 para" id="v87992267" data-testid="topicPara"><span data-testid="topicText" class="TopicPara_topicText__G6F4C">Providing daily or at least every other day toothbrushing appears to be the most effective way to improve and maintain periodontal health. In addition, dental diets; chews, treats, and toys; oral gels, rinses, and sprays; and water and food additives can be used to help stabilize and decrease the incidence of periodontal disease.</span></p></li></ul></div></section><span id="references" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992268" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><span id="v87992269" class="TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionWithoutScroll__v86E8" tabindex="-1" data-testid="topicFHeadAnchor" aria-hidden="true"></span><section id="References_v87992268" class=" veterinary TopicFHead_topicFHeadSectionIdWithoutScroll__68joZ"><h2 class="TopicFHead_topicFHead___lHdE
